Like I'll end up being slower moving around the cars at the shop, or... you know, up and down the court. \n\nSo, I just prefer to keep things how they are.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight affect what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I'm under cars all day at the shop, so... I can't really afford to be slower moving around. Or up and down the court, either. It's just better to keep things where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I don't really see what the big deal is.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"afraid\" is the right word for it. \n\nIt's just... well, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or... if my jeans start feeling tighter, I just don't like it. It makes me think I'm getting soft. Like I'll end up being slower moving around the cars at the shop, or... you know, up and down the court. \n\nSo, I just prefer to keep things how they are.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight affect what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I just... eat what I eat. I don't really sit around thinking about it that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever choose not to eat something because you're worried it might make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... worrying. I just have my routine. \n\nMost days I keep it to coffee in the morning. Maybe a small sandwich, or... just half of whatever's around later on. Between the shop running late and things being busy at the house, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. It's just easier that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because they might make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't... I don't really look at it like that. \n\nI just don't eat a lot of junk or heavy stuff, I guess. I don't sit around thinking about a list of things I can't have. I just... I know what works for me, and I stick to what I usually have. It's not a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you eat more than you usually do?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say anxious. It's just... I guess if I overdo it, and the scale bumps up even a pound, or my work jeans start feeling tighter... I just don't like it. \n\nIt makes me think I'm getting soft. I'm under cars all day at the shop, so... I can't really afford to be slower moving around. Or up and down the court, either. It's just better to keep things where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Like I just said... it's not really about worrying. \n\nIf I eat more, the scale goes up. My work pants get tight. I just don't want to get soft and start slowing down at the shop, or on the court. That's all it is. I don't really sit around thinking about 'what if' I did things differently. I just keep it how it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about gaining weight when you're deciding what to eat?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I guess. Like I was just saying... I just don't want the scale to bump up. \n\nIf I eat the wrong thing and my jeans get tight, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I can't afford to be slow around the cars or on the court. So... yeah, I just stick to what I know keeps me where I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating less because you're afraid of what might happen if you gain weight?\n\nPatient: Look, I feel like we keep going in circles here. I wouldn't use the word afraid. \n\nIt's just... like I keep telling you, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or my jeans get tight, I know I'm getting soft. I can't be slower moving around the cars at work, or on the court. So yeah, I guess I keep my food down so that doesn't happen. It's just practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still limit your food intake if you weren't playing basketball?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Hard to say. I haven't really thought about it. \n\nI still have my job at the shop to think about, so... probably. I don't plan on quitting basketball anytime soon anyway, so I don't really see a reason to guess about it. I just like keeping things the way they are.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And, uh... I don't feel as weighed down when I'm working at the garage. But... yeah. That's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Did you try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I was trying to, no. It's just... most days I keep it to coffee in the morning. Then maybe a small sandwich later on, or... just half of whatever's around. \n\nBetween the shop running late and the house being so busy, it's just easier. I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight loss is a good thing?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a... a good thing. It just is what it is. \n\nI know some folks are getting worried about it, but... I figure they're making way too much of it, honestly. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day. I take care of whatever needs doing at home. I still play ball on the weekends. So... yeah. I don't really see what the big deal is.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"afraid\" is the right word for it. \n\nIt's just... well, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or... if my jeans start feeling tighter, I just don't like it. It makes me think I'm getting soft. Like I'll end up being slower moving around the cars at the shop, or... you know, up and down the court. \n\nSo, I just prefer to keep things how they are.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight affect what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I just... eat what I eat. I don't really sit around thinking about it that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever choose not to eat something because you're worried it might make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... worrying. I just have my routine. \n\nMost days I keep it to coffee in the morning. Maybe a small sandwich, or... just half of whatever's around later on. Between the shop running late and things being busy at the house, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. It's just easier that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because they might make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't... I don't really look at it like that. \n\nI just don't eat a lot of junk or heavy stuff, I guess. I don't sit around thinking about a list of things I can't have. I just... It's just... I guess if I overdo it, and the scale bumps up even a pound, or my work jeans start feeling tighter... I just don't like it. \n\nIt makes me think I'm getting soft. I just don't want the scale to bump up. \n\nIf I eat the wrong thing and my jeans get tight, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I can't afford to be slow around the cars or on the court. I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight loss is a good thing?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a... a good thing. It just is what it is. \n\nI know some folks are getting worried about it, but... I figure they're making way too much of it, honestly. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day. I take care of whatever needs doing at home. I still play ball on the weekends. So... yeah. I don't really see what the big deal is.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"afraid\" is the right word for it. \n\nIt's just... well, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or... if my jeans start feeling tighter, I just don't like it. It makes me think I'm getting soft. Like I'll end up being slower moving around the cars at the shop, or... you know, up and down the court. \n\nSo, I just prefer to keep things how they are.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight affect what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I just... eat what I eat. I don't really sit around thinking about it that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever choose not to eat something because you're worried it might make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... worrying. I just have my routine. \n\nMost days I keep it to coffee in the morning. Maybe a small sandwich, or... just half of whatever's around later on. Between the shop running late and things being busy at the house, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. It's just easier that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because they might make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't... I don't really look at it like that. \n\nI just don't eat a lot of junk or heavy stuff, I guess. I don't sit around thinking about a list of things I can't have. I just... I know what works for me, and I stick to what I usually have. It's not a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you eat more than you usually do?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say anxious. It's just... I guess if I overdo it, and the scale bumps up even a pound, or my work jeans start feeling tighter... I just don't like it. \n\nIt makes me think I'm getting soft. I'm under cars all day at the shop, so... I can't really afford to be slower moving around. Or up and down the court, either. It's just better to keep things where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Like I just said... it's not really about worrying. \n\nIf I eat more, the scale goes up. My work pants get tight. I just don't want to get soft and start slowing down at the shop, or on the court. That's all it is. I don't really sit around thinking about 'what if' I did things differently. I just keep it how it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about gaining weight when you're deciding what to eat?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I guess. Like I was just saying... I just don't want the scale to bump up. \n\nIf I eat the wrong thing and my jeans get tight, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I can't afford to be slow around the cars or on the court. So... yeah, I just stick to what I know keeps me where I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating less because you're afraid of what might happen if you gain weight?\n\nPatient: Look, I feel like we keep going in circles here. I wouldn't use the word afraid. \n\nIt's just... like I keep telling you, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or my jeans get tight, I know I'm getting soft. I can't be slower moving around the cars at work, or on the court. So yeah, I guess I keep my food down so that doesn't happen. It's just practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still limit your food intake if you weren't playing basketball?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Hard to say. I haven't really thought about it. \n\nI still have my job at the shop to think about, so... probably. I don't plan on quitting basketball anytime soon anyway, so I don't really see a reason to guess about it. I just like keeping things the way they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to keep your weight the same if you didn't have to be physically active for work or basketball?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really know how to answer that. \n\nIt's just a lot of \"what ifs.\" I work in a shop, and I play. That's my life. So... I haven't really sat around thinking about some made-up scenario where I don't do those things. I just... I prefer being the size I am. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to keep your weight low if you weren't playing basketball and didn't work in a physically demanding job\n\nPatient: I don't know why we're still on this. Like I said... I haven't thought about it. \n\nI just... I am who I am. I do what I do. I don't see the point in guessing about a life I don't have. I just want to stay the way I am now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to limit your food intake if you weren't playing basketball or working in a physically demanding job?\n\nPatient: Look, doc... we keep going in circles here. I don't know. \n\nI'm not trying to be difficult, I just... I don't live in a \"what if\" world. I fix cars. I play ball. I eat what I eat. I don't know what else you want me to say about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to be thin if it didn't affect your performance in basketball or at work?\n\nPatient: I... man, you're asking the exact same thing again. I don't have a different answer for you. \n\nI just... I like being this size.
